- Laptop budget: 16 GB. No parallel agents, app servers, builds, test suites or simulators. One current-app environment plus essential support services. Use one build/test worker where supported.
- Founder constraint added 2026-11-06: **all live inference tests across every application must explicitly select Luna**. Do not use Auto or another live model to exercise a case. Fixtures and unit tests are not provider calls; model IDs in product source still come from the catalog. The earlier Q6R8 request used Auto before this instruction and its actual receipt was Luna; subsequent requests must use the named selection.
- Latest founder constraints: **local authentication uses only `http://localhost:3000`**, and testing uses the existing **demo@agiworkforce.com** account. Do not create additional testing accounts. Clerk CLI is authorized for the verification/sign-in setup. Keep credentials out of files and evidence. Earlier lane-chrome results below are historical; do not continue with that account or port 3001.
- Routine browser actions already within the task, including filling/next/closing finished test tabs through Chrome’s X, are authorized. Do them directly without a repeated chat confirmation; retain the task’s consequential-action and account/site boundaries.
- Preflight on 2026-11-06 found no running application dev server, Chrome/Chromium process, active application build/test suite or Simulator UI. Local Postgres and Redis are already listening. Idle OS/CoreSimulator services and user-owned unrelated programs were not killed.
- Agent-browser `0.41.2` is installed as an external QA tool (Apache-2.0, `vercel-labs/agent-browser`); it is not vendored into product code. Stable worktree session: `agi-launch-chrome-413342fae3e5`. Do not launch a second session alongside it.
- Passive Playwright CDP instrumentation must use supported `{ noDefaults: true }` when attaching to this user-operated Chrome. The installed default applies download/focus/media overrides; its first file test redirected the download to a deleted temporary artifact. Native download behavior was explicitly restored before the qualified repeat. Do not infer product download failures or saved-file success from those artifacts.
- Network artifact counts labelled model requests cover the chat-completions endpoint, unless the artifact says otherwise. They do not include separate server chat-title inference. The current usage reconciliation includes all new managed ledger rows, including titles; a no-post-Stop claim from those endpoint-only observers means no later chat-completion/browser-agent request, not no auxiliary title generation. Current primary/title receipts both resolve to named Luna.
- Real workflow acceptance requires the actual application and observable output. Unit/mocked/harness-only results do not close a user workflow. Record failures and permissions as well as success.
- Prior passing cases below retain their original date/scope. Do not repeat them when implementation and relevant dependencies are unchanged. Inspect changed owner functions and queue only affected cases. A changed bundle hash alone is not a reason to repeat every case.
- Authentication/pairing working-tree changes after the prior Chrome checks invalidate those boundaries; they do not erase unrelated native-field engine evidence.
- Do not expose secrets in commands, logs, screenshots or this ledger. Test data/accounts must be development/test and isolated from production. No production mutation or public store submission is recorded or implied.

## Confirmed fixes and cross-application review

- CH-ENV-001: corrected only ignored Chrome API/sync overrides, deriving their value from the existing web localhost origin. Inspected VS Code user-scoped apiEndpoint, Mobile EXPO_PUBLIC_API_URL, Electron trusted AGI_CLOUD_APP_ORIGIN, Tauri VITE_API_BASE_URL and CLI caller/API auth-base owners. No equivalent two-key mismatch was confirmed; their local setup/runtime stays deferred.
- CH-ENV-004: extracted the existing Clerk session mapping/list/get/revoke into one shared manager. Web still obtains its request-scoped Next Clerk client through the existing retry/cache path; the development CLI receives a backend-only manager using its already validated development secret. Deprovision policy, bounded revocation and tenant restrictions are unchanged. The organization-shared connector cache has one framework-independent owner; discovery reexports its original eviction API and deprovision imports that owner without loading the entire MCP runtime. Other application source has no direct dependency on the Next identity adapter or this server-only cache; all cloud clients still use the existing backend contract. Their account/cleanup integration checks remain deferred by stage.
- Resource setup: both Chrome Vite passes share `terserOptions.maxWorkers = 1`; actual builds for the initial setup, auth fix and changed :3000 configuration passed serially. No other app build was started.
- CH-AUTH-001: `watchSyncHostSession` now invalidates its cached Clerk client on an actual changed cookie value, before the existing debounced notification. Same-value rewrites still do nothing. Both sign-in and sign-out cache transitions pass. Reviewed Web's provider-session refs, VS Code's SecretStorage/device-session refresh, Mobile's current Clerk session minter, Electron's hosted Web/session proxy, Tauri's native sign-in/session-token transport, CLI's managed-cloud renewal and shared identity ownership. No equivalent Chrome sync-cookie cache exists there; no speculative source changes were made, and their runtime checks remain deferred.
- CH-PERF-001: tool-capable explicit current-page/form requests reuse the existing permitted capture before the first model request. Shared formObservationReference binds the observed document/snapshot to the current assistant. Capture rejects an account/stream/page change; retry keeps live-capture intent and refuses stale data after lost permission. Optional empty-DOM prefetch retains the existing browser-tool fallback; explicit page attachment semantics stay required. Native batches now return a full-plan verified boolean and verifiedValue only for successfully retained native controls; select receipts use the visible label, not hidden option IDs. Protected/failed controls have no verifiedValue. A newly failing queued-input-handler test exposed verification happening too early; one event-loop yield now precedes the batch's verification. Existing native setters/events/permissions/limits remain intact.
- Source review for CH-PERF-001 covered Web/hosted Electron browser context, VS Code context handoff, Mobile service inputs, Tauri native bridge, CLI cloud attachments and the shared Playwright browser-tool snapshot. None use Chrome's private turn-capture/form-snapshot contract, so no speculative equivalent patches were made. Chrome's generic and supported-ATS contact paths both reuse the changed batch owner. The shared CDP reader work affects Chrome browser observations and any native bridge consumer; other-app runtime verification remains deferred.
- CH-PERF-002: extracted one closure-free DOM element reader shared by content capture and isolated CDP serialization. Associated native/ARIA labels and checkbox/toggle state are exposed without reading protected input values. Initial agent context is DOM-only; screenshots are explicit fallback. Fresh guarded DOM attaches after an entire executed action batch as tool evidence, preserving intra-batch indices. Context pruning now preserves text-only goal and action receipt prefixes when old DOM expires. The first live changed-path failure proved this regression; final real-task readback passed. Cross-surface source inspection found Web/hosted Electron protects the latest user message in context-window.ts, VS Code delegates compaction to its CLI runtime, Mobile uses shared context budgeting, and Tauri delegates to shared compaction. None use this Chrome DOM-history owner; no equivalent defect was confirmed or speculative change made. Those runtimes remain deferred.
- CH-ACT-005: cancellation prefers a freshly resolved credential only for the exact admitted account/session; an ambient different owner never replaces its captured credential. Stop state prevents a dismissed tool approval from resuming inference. Chrome activity reconciliation now consumes the canonical lifecycle projection for ready_for_review, timed_out and archived rather than keeping an incomplete private terminal-name check. Web/hosted Electron and Mobile run clients resolve auth callbacks at operation time; Tauri reads its current token under a goal-owner boundary and CLI has session renewal in its HTTP owner. VS Code's cloud-task detail client snapshots a bearer before an interactive picker; token-lifetime/renewal and owner behavior need a stage-specific review before calling that counterpart equivalent or verified. Its source/runtime are not claimed fixed. Mobile and VS Code already present ready_for_review as settled; no equivalent spinner change was confirmed there. Other-app runtimes remain deferred.
- CH-TABS-001 is a Chrome-window utility in trusted extension UI, reusing the existing picker and notice styles. The selected snapshot is rechecked before every close; account/chat changes stop remaining work. The native utility has no page/model message entrypoint and does not relax TaskTabs' created-by-this-task guard. No shared or other-app browser-tab owner was changed. Official tabs API reference refreshed 2026-11-06: https://developer.chrome.com/docs/extensions/reference/api/tabs. README, store-listing permission justification and threat model describe this added local action.
- CH-PERF-003 portable-reader review: Chrome native bridge and desktop/CLI paired-browser commands retain their current owners; only the Chrome agent consumes getPageContent. Web/hosted Electron, VS Code and Mobile do not import this private reader. The separate shared Playwright snapshot uses role locators/textContent and does not currently include control values; assess its field-readback behavior in its consuming app stage rather than treating this Chrome pass as coverage. No other-app runtime or speculative equivalent patch is claimed.
- References refreshed 2026-11-06: Stagehand's official observe/act pattern (https://github.com/browserbase/stagehand/blob/main/packages/docs/v3/references/act.mdx), MIT license (https://github.com/browserbase/stagehand/blob/main/LICENSE), and Claude's official browser-use state/batch contract (https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/agents-and-tools/tool-use/browser-use-tool). These are documented reference patterns, not competitor UI observations. Reused the existing native/Playwright/CDP architecture; no Stagehand code or remote service was imported and no new dependency was added. No third-party code-copy attribution is being claimed.
- Local database setup used the existing local administrator only to create the isolated database owned by the application role and install the required pgvector extension. Migrations ran as the existing application role. The first migration attempt stopped at the extension privilege requirement; the wrapper was corrected to propagate `main()`'s return code, setup resumed through the supported runner, and `verify` proved the complete ledger. A partial lane-chrome seed before that failure was repaired by the guarded reset after schema completion. No existing account content/database or production data was reset.

## Open inherited verification failures

The Nov 3 full suite had five failures in untracked chrome-subscription.test.ts and task-tabs-background.test.ts. Reproduced all five on Nov 6, then corrected the tests against executable billing capabilities and the advertised open-and-switch tool contract. Chrome ordinary chat uses managed_chat (Free/Basic included), not developer_surfaces (CLI/VS Code); admitted mocked chat, AGI Work denial and unadmitted-model denial are now distinct tests. Server refusals exercise the public stream instead of importing the private accountRefusal helper. Numeric usage counter exclusion assertions remain. Task fixtures now model Chrome activation and additionally prove changed-target and failed-activation refusals. All 138 tests across these two files plus managed-chat-handler and free-trial-quota pass; scoped test lint passes. These are fixture/contract corrections, not new entitlement or tab runtime acceptance, and no full-suite pass is claimed. The prior web typecheck's untracked missing usage export remains outside this Chrome UI/feature scope.

## 2026-11-08 user-requested hook alignment

- User explicitly requested adjusting hooks to current code. Found a real pre-commit instrument bug: --staged chose index paths but read working copies, allowing staged unsafe code to hide behind an unstaged safe copy and falsely rejecting a safe staged copy when the working file was bad. New staged reader freezes blob IDs from Git index metadata, reads those immutable blobs (including taxonomy/ratchet/lock metadata), rejects an index that changes during validation, and handles exact NUL-delimited filenames. Git calls use argument arrays and fail closed. Output is bounded16MiB with concise failures that do not dump Git stdout.

Current official storage/lifecycle reference, checked2026-11-09: [Chrome storage areas](https://developer.chrome.com/docs/extensions/reference/api/storage#session) documents that session storage survives worker dormancy but is cleared on extension disable/reload/update or browser restart; [worker lifecycle](https://developer.chrome.com/docs/extensions/develop/concepts/service-workers/lifecycle) requires persisted state across worker termination. Therefore use panel close/reopen or a separately qualified worker stop for the pending intent case, not whole-extension reload. These are documented lifecycle limits, not an observed pending-request restart pass.

## Caller conversation and workspace authority: 2026-11-09

Previous turn was progress on individual caller-tool receipts. Full objective reread; Chrome remains current. CH-CALLER-CONVERSATION-AUTHORITY-01: runtime resume/approval/input messages now carry the local history conversation ID. Caller custody retains an optional acknowledged conversation-context snapshot: cloud conversation ID, explicit organization ID or personal null, and project ID. Capture uses canonical owner-filtered local history and requires acknowledged cloud binding with a synced project; normal admission adds no network/model read for this snapshot. A different outbound cloud ID or later re-admission under another project/organization refuses. Missing original organization proof is not silently normalized to personal. callerConversationAuthority reuses the existing history workspace reader, owner-fenced cloud chat client and shared20s history read budget. It snapshots input before awaits, checks operation/cancellation, compares local binding, checks current account/workspace, reads the original remote conversation under its explicit organization header (limit1), rejects remote project/organization/temporary mismatches, then rechecks active workspace and local binding. This prevents adopting stale local metadata after a remote move. No history/project/workspace is mutated. It stores only IDs; no credentials or message bodies are added to custody. The dependency type exposes only getConversation rather than casting an incomplete client to the full SDK.

Background resume and hosted approval/input invoke this authority before caller continuation/receipt posting whenever a negotiated checkpoint or captured conversation scope exists. Legacy server-only approvals without a browser checkpoint/scope keep their existing path; they do not gain browser dispatch. Scope-aware legacy records are checked. Original account/session, website, permission and receipt checks remain. First-party negotiation stays off.

The broker reads the server journal before posting anything: completed receipts are reposted only when the server still waits at that exact checkpoint. Already-completed runs do not repost, and a later checkpoint advances local custody under its existing cursor/step budget. A posted-cursor set refuses repeated unacknowledged receipt loops. Unknown/legacy claims remain refused for execution after read-only server inspection, rather than being treated as unstarted.
